## Research

My research and teaching interests focus mainly on understanding how the human body is used as a communication device\. At the core of this embodied approach are the basic physical mechanisms of movement control and sensation and their interactions with communication, including speech production, perception, control, phonetics and phonology in normal, disordered, and children’s speech, signed languages, singing and other musical and emotional expression\. More broadly, I am interested in how people interact with each other and their environment, and how this interaction can inform models of linguistic communication and movement control\. Within this paradigm, some of my major research programs have focused on techniques for applying ultrasound imaging technology to speech research, protocols for studying haptic \(tactile\) and multimodal perception of speech, methods for observing aspects of speech motor behaviour such as planning and posture, and biomechanical simulation of speech \(see [www\.artisynth\.org](http://artisynth.magic.ubc.ca/artisynth/)\)\.

[Integrated Speech Research Lab](https://blogs.ubc.ca/isrl/)

## Teaching

---

## Research

My research and teaching interests focus mainly on understanding how the human body is used as a communication device\. At the core of this embodied approach are the basic physical mechanisms of movement control and sensation and their interactions with communication, including speech production, perception, control, phonetics and phonology in normal, disordered, and children’s speech, signed languages, singing and other musical and emotional expression\. More broadly, I am interested in how people interact with each other and their environment, and how this interaction can inform models of linguistic communication and movement control\. Within this paradigm, some of my major research programs have focused on techniques for applying ultrasound imaging technology to speech research, protocols for studying haptic \(tactile\) and multimodal perception of speech, methods for observing aspects of speech motor behaviour such as planning and posture, and biomechanical simulation of speech \(see [www\.artisynth\.org](http://artisynth.magic.ubc.ca/artisynth/)\)\.

[Integrated Speech Research Lab](https://blogs.ubc.ca/isrl/)

Research *keyboard\_arrow\_down*

My research and teaching interests focus mainly on understanding how the human body is used as a communication device\. At the core of this embodied approach are the basic physical mechanisms of movement control and sensation and their interactions with communication, including speech production, perception, control, phonetics and phonology in normal, disordered, and children’s speech, signed languages, singing and other musical and emotional expression\. More broadly, I am interested in how people interact with each other and their environment, and how this interaction can inform models of linguistic communication and movement control\. Within this paradigm, some of my major research programs have focused on techniques for applying ultrasound imaging technology to speech research, protocols for studying haptic \(tactile\) and multimodal perception of speech, methods for observing aspects of speech motor behaviour such as planning and posture, and biomechanical simulation of speech \(see [www\.artisynth\.org](http://artisynth.magic.ubc.ca/artisynth/)\)\.

[Integrated Speech Research Lab](https://blogs.ubc.ca/isrl/)
